IT IS THE ONE THAT MOST ADAPTABTLE TO CHANGE CHARLES DARWIN QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Smimon's Journal #549 smimon Offline Trader Posts: 549 Thank you received: 1075 I've been live for around 3 or 4 months now (can't remember the exact date). I'm pretty broke, so have only been able to fund a micro account. In that time, I have blown the account 3 times, and had to redeposit funds. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=525 36/41 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 22 - RTM Community Am I losing 75% of my trades? Far from it. I win around 80%, some days I don't even HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT take a loss, but then I get offside badly on a single trade sequence, refuse to admit I am wrong, and get margin called. I'm probably now on my 5th or 6th cycle of "double, treble or even quadruple my account in a 2-3 week period with a 80%+ win rate, and then hand it all back again with one stupid trade sequence". Naturally, this has to stop. It's absurd to win 80% of your trades, and then blow your account. One of the main reasons I've been able to keep the win rate so high, has been not using a SL. By only entering at important larger levels, and keeping the big picture in mind, it's been possible to average out of 95% of my bad entries - but then there's that 5% that break, and never come back... Sometimes, price is just on an uber trend, and will blow through levels you could have sworn should have given a decent reaction with no more than a 5 pip flicker. I believe that with more experience, I'd probably be able to understand when price is in this sort of mood, and step aside. But it's only been just over a year since I found RTM, and although I am good at this point, I am not that good. And I've got bills to pay, soon. One plus that has come out of not using a SL, it that is has really sharpened up my analysis skills - I only try and trade zones where there was another obvious cluster nearby that could bounce and let me out if things went wrong. In the process of trading like this, I learned that a lot of the most profitable trades come not from trading a level directly, but from trading the FO of the level. I've also become extremely tuned in to M1 PA, and can recognise a reversal forming usually before the candle is even finished. Put it this way - how many times does price respect a valid level, as expected? And how many times does it either FO, or engulf it but immediately begin to RT afterwards? I'd say the latter scenario happens about 4 times more often. When you think about it in terms of ITs needing LQ for their positions, they aren't going to get much when price makes a valid bounce from a very obvious FTR. When price breaks above (or below) and comes off some random cluster instead though? They'll get tons. And with time and experience, you can spot these seemingly "unimportant" clusters well ahead of time, and be ready. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=525 37/41 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 22 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Now let me preface this next section with the following - this is not a whine, or a rant, and I am fully responsible for my own decisions I've made. I am also eternally thankful to this community for teaching me what I need to know to make money out of the markets. With that out of the way, there is one small issue I'd like to highlight about RTM, and member's journals - almost no-one is honest about how many they lose or posts up their losing trades more than once in a blue moon. (this goes for me too of course) I think the reason for this is obvious - we're putting ourselves on public display in a community of our peers, and our own egos coupled with a desire to earn the respect of said peers means we generally only post up our trades when we are winning. It's fine, and it's natural. However - this creates a pretty toxic false impression that everyone else except you is winning all the time. This leads you into thoughts of "What am I doing wrong? Why do I lose so many? What do they know that I don't?" In truth, I have probably been good enough to make a ton of cash from the markets for 6 months now. But I have become obsessed with never taking a loss. Things like when IF posts up a screenshot of someone with 125 wins in a row doesn't help someone like me - I am competitive and a perfectionist, and I firmly believe that anything anyone else can do, I can do if I put my mind to it. All of these weeks and months of trading with no SL, all the time, have been as a direct result of me going on an ego trip needing to never be wrong, and under the impression that it is possible to win every single trade, and that there are a bunch of "high level" people on the forum doing it day in, day out. Shadowpit yesterday had the candour to admit that he loses 50% of his trades, for small risk - but losses nonetheless. Which directly led to me sitting here thinking "What the hell am I doing wiping out my account every 3 weeks, when all I just need to do is take a win or take a loss, and move on?" https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=525 38/41 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 22 - RTM Community I don't want to blow my own trumpet, but I am really good now at finding price turning HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT points that react within 1-2 pips. If I were to just take these levels with a tight SL on M1 - a lot of the time I could be making 20R+ several times over the course of the day. What then does it matter if I lose 5 trades? So anyway this is just a cathartic tirade, but the upshot of it is from next week, I will go back to using a SL, and take my profits when I get them, and the losses too, in the full knowledge that I will end up massively up on average if I just stick to my guns. It's going to be a bit of a learning curve again - I need to learn how to stay calm, and not become emotional after losing a few trades in a row, which was traditionally a big problem for me. ThLiOnkGCOAUDTJPY is about to go long? Quickly check UCAD - is it at a level it could go short from? Great, take the trade. Is it in the middle of a PAZ? Probably better wait. As discussed previously, individual instruments do not exist in a vacuum. The Fx market is driven by money flowing from one set of currencies into others. It is no coincidence that when an entire currency is about to make a turn, it's major and minors are usually all at significant levels at the same time. In this way we can also usually get a good guess at which directions large news announcements are going to go - 5-10 mins out from GBP news for example, go check the big picture on all GBP crosses, see if they are sat at important levels, or have just turned from important levels 30 mins ago etc. If you're looking at a setup on an individual minor crosspair, and none of the other crosses for this currency are reflecting the same PA or are at good levels, then you should probably wait. I wrote this indi a little while back to help me out with this - it enables me to quickly look at all crosses for a given currency at once, without needing the chart open. It inverts those crosses that need inverting, so that they're all the correct way up with respect to the currency being viewed. i.imgur.com/jBYu4Nx.png The following user(s) said Thank You: ZeeDeveel QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Smimon's Journal #554 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=550 5/41 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 23 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT jon Offline Trader Posts: 5 Thank you received: 2 I remember reading a post by TheRealThing at FF many years ago and what he wrote has stuck with me. In my view it is just a way to observe the current sentiment, just as higher highs, higher lows, price moving higher, price moving lower will do the same. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=550 9/41 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 23 - RTM Community QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE FORUM MORE LOGOUT HOME EDUCATION THANK YOU Smimon's Journal #558 smimon Offline Trader Posts: 549 Thank you received: 1075 Certainly, it's just a single piece of information out of many that must be taken into consideration. But the engulf is one of our main weapons of determining how far a move can potentially go. The theory being that there are key price levels in the market, as defined by important FTRs (amongst other things), that contain many orders, which are defended. Once those orders are removed, price is therefore free to travel to the next important cluster, or the edge of the next PAZ. The confusion comes when you realise there are many more types of level price can make a valid bounce from, not just FTRs. In this way, an important FTR can appear to be engulfed, but in fact price has made a valid turn from another type of level. The more that price FOs, the more confusing this picture becomes, and the harder it is to know exactly how far we can hold a trade. Higher highs and lower lows are by themselves somewhat meaningless unless viewed in the context of FTRs/PAZ. A higher high that was rejected from MPL of an important bearish FTR is in fact a reversal signal, not a continuation signal for instance. The following user(s) said Thank You: jon 10/41 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=550 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 23 - RTM Community QUICKHROEPMLYE REPELYDUCQAUTOITOEN FORUM MORE LOGOUT THANK YOU Smimon's Journal #559 smimon Offline Trader Posts: 549 Thank you received: 1075 Ideally, when we enter a trade, we want to see price engulf the nearest important level in our favour. This is then our cue to try and hold the trade further. The harder it is to define a valid engulf however, the harder it is to have confidence to understand where price may be headed next. I then fit DPs on SR etc into the context of these FTRs. If the structure of the FTR appears to support it, I will attempt a trade from near MPL, otherwise I start looking for seemingly unimportant DPs on S/D levels that price can FO into or react from - but it's always in the context of important higher scale FTRs - these are our best roadmap of what price wants to do. We then fit the IT tricks in and around these. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Smimon's Journal #563 Posts: 549 smimon Offline Trader 18/41 Thank you received: 1075 Example - GJ short I took today i.imgur.com/MyECRTi.png https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=550 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 23 - RTM Community After a while, it's just the same old tricks, over and over again. The key is to learn to HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT recognise: - which levels are likely to get ignored/faked, and where they are going to fake into - which important FTRs are good to trade from the near the edge without them breaking I prefer the first scenario personally, because then I am either trading a reversal from genuine FO, or I am trading the RT cycle after an eng of an important level which can be large sometimes. A valid reaction to a valid levels happens far less often than the combined amount of times a FO or eng + RT happens. The second scenario is totally valid however, and works well a lot of the time. The key to recognising which FTR edges are more likely to work is to recognise the smaller scale structure near the edge, and where it is located in relation to the next significant orders. This comes largely from experience, but generally you want to see something on smaller scale that can be faked, and something for it to fake into. Remember the ITs can't generate much LQ from turning price at a massively obvious level - if you can see if that obviously, so can the rest of the world. When considering the trade of any level, I now first ask myself - how much LQ can the ITs generate at this level? A lot of traders are a lot more S/D savvy than they used to be. A valid turn from an obvious valid level means the ITs aren't going to get too many of their orders filled for big positions. Instead, when considering a level, it's always a good idea to look nearby, and see if there is a better level that price was turned just before, such as this example here in the PA that is developing from the move up on GN. No guarantees this will work here - but the presence of that swing low "hovering" above the QM gives a great low risk/high RR opportunity upon return. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Smimon's Journal #591 smimon Offline Trader Posts: 549 Thank you received: 1075 Yes maknill, you are correct. I had it in my mind that is was a "rule" that price was not allowed to do a continuation FO when it was coming off a valid HTF level - I thought continuation engulfs (FO of minor level and continue) could occur only when there was a HTF engulf in favour of the move in play - but I guess this is not a rule after all There is a fine balance to be struck between application of a strict price model to enable us to make reasonable assumptions and predictions of price movement, but also when to understand that these are not set in stone and that rules can be bent (or sometimes broken totally). https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=575 25/44 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 24 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Imagine a world with no FOs... how easy would all this be QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Smimon's Journal #592 smimon Offline Trader Posts: 549 Thank you received: 1075 If any of you are anything like me, you probably have markup on your charts all the time, showing zones of interest, marking where engulfs occurred, etc. Indeed, I've even written a ton of indicators to help me out with this, including a FlagLimit indicator which allows FLs to be defined on multiple scales, finds if they have been engulfed or not, monitors them for price proximity/enter/engulf etc. Since I like to adopt a currency based approach to my trading, I trade all USD and EUR crosses, in conjunction with 7 currency indexes (I don't trade CHF crosses just because the spread on most of them is terrible (apart from ECHF and UCHF) and because the index confluence doesn't work too well). This means I've got over 15 charts open across 2 instances of MT4, all with marked zones and alerts on them. At any given time, especially in times of high volatility in the markets, there are alerts going off every 2 minutes. You can have been waiting patiently for a setup, and watching price, and suddenly another 4 alerts go off, and your concentration is pulled all over the place. My head is a mess, my thinking is muddled. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=575 26/44 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 24 - RTM Community I am also guilty of having a proximity alert to one of my zones go off, and then just HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT thinking "I'll go stare at something else, and wait for the alert to go off that price has actually entered the zone before I decide whether to hit it or not". As a result, when price does enter the zone, I've not been paying attention, and I am out of tune with the approach, the speed of it, the way the candles are forming etc, and I tend to just make a rushed hasty decision to hit the level without a second thought. Sometimes it's fine if I've analysed the zone correctly, sometimes it goes instantly wrong as I've misjudged the strength of the move. Also, whether you are aware of it or not, the second you place a single piece of markup on your charts, a single line, box, whatever - your thinking is influenced from that point forwards. Every time you look at that chart, you are starting with a single default assumption based on your markup, and everything subsequently is viewed through this lens. If your markup is valid and accurate - great; but if it's not, everything you try to trade from that point forwards will be biased towards being incorrect. Think - how many times have you drawn a zone, and then later in the day some more price action plays out, and you realised it was totally incorrect? All of this is to say - less is more. I've realised that the only thing holding me back from consistent success (my equity is up and down like a yo-yo at present) is my mental approach and organisation. I need a clear mind, need to be able to focus, need to be reading price clearly every time I sit down and not base my thinking on a load of assumptions I made 2 days ago. To this end, I now only set alerts on my currency index charts (crosspairs don't usually make turns until the relevant currency index is at the correct level anyway), and at the start of every day, I wipe every single object off my charts. This cleaning of the charts has 2 benefits - it not only makes sure you are really reading what's going on with no previous assumptions, it also over time will dramatically improve your speed of analysis by eye, until you get to the point where you can just eyeball a chart and know pretty much what's going on in under 10 seconds, and where the next likely levels in play will be. I also now do not allow myself to use "zone entered" alerts - if I receive a proximity alert for a zone, i turn everything else off, and force myself to stare at that chart until the setup plays out. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=575 27/44 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 24 - RTM Community You can glean a lot more information out of a naked chart than a chart with 43 boxes HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT and lines all over it. Don't forget, the manner in which price is moving (surging, driving, compressing, flagging etc) is one of the biggest clues we have as to future intent, or whether what we think is an engulf is actually a FO. Yoy simple cannot see this piece of information clearly with clutter everywhere. Every time I sit down, I'm not only analysing charts, I'm also trying to fight my base personality. I assume ownership of my failure, and admit that the reason I'm not succeeding is due to my own failings. This is something we call intellectual honesty. Of course, the other option is to protect your ego, and tell yourself some pretty lie that the reason you didn't succeed with a proven method is not because you personally failed to apply it properly, but because the teacher is "BS". Don't forget people - trading is HARD. There is pretty much zero chance you're going to pick this up and run with consistent profits in less than 12 months, at an absolute minimum. Sure, you can gain the analysis skills in much less time than that, but learning to control your own mental state and fight those elements of your brain that are https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=575 41/44 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 24 - RTM Community constantly striving to sabotage you - well, that's the real battle, and that's what takes HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT years of endless repetition. You also need this amount of time to really develop that "feel" for price, which cannot be quantified. I've added 1000% to my account over a 12 day period. I've added 100% to my account even in a single day. But then I've gone on a madness revenge spiral and given it all back, or blown my account with a single stupid trade with no SL, which I kept adding to all the way. As soon as I can stop self-sabotaging, my consistent returns and large sums of money are waiting for me. The only variable in the equation I still need to solve is me. A "favourite" of mine - I am bored waiting for price to reach a level I want to trade, so I start scalping silly midrange PA on M1 in times of low volatility (M1 PA is much tougher to read when the market is slow). These are the times of real danger, because your conscious mind is not fully focused on what you are doing, and you can quite easily end up doing strange things that you wouldn't normally have dreamed of doing. I'm learning to spot when my mind has started drifting, and this is my cue to take a break and refocus. The following user(s) said Thank You: smimon QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Smimon's Journal #610 geejay Offline Trader PRICE ACTION WILL SHOW YOU THE WAY Posts: 41 Thank you received: 63 Art wrote: Partial close is such a huge help. Have been doing it for quite some time now and works like charm with putting one at ease. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=600 14/34 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 25 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT I just wanted to add that this too is saving my backside. And in my hubris, I think I have touch traded the bottom or top of an H4 swing and then next thing I know I am stopped out, watching a +5R or more trade turn into a -1R. So, what I do is take 50% profit at 1R or the closest logical bounce point. That covers my risk completely. If I do get stopped out, it is very close to breakeven. Then if I do catch a nice swing move, I can let price take me out at a higher time frame Flag or FTR. And note: I do NOT change my Stop Loss. I leave it in place and simply take enough profits to cover any loss if I do get stopped out later. I realize that this seems like a 'crutch' but it is my coping mechanism at the moment and it gives me peace to know that while I may not be fully mature at reading price, I can still make trades and learn successful money management skills at the same time. GJ QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Smimon's Journal #611 Posts: 549 smimon Offline Trader Thank you received: 1075 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=600 15/34 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 25 - RTM Community geejaHyOwMroEte: I realEizDeUthCaAt tThIisOsNeems likFeOaR'cUrMutch' but Mit iOs RmEy copingLOGOUT mechanism at the moment and it gives me peace to know that while I may not be fully mature at reading price, I can still make trades and learn successful money management skills at the same time. Not in the slightest, there's no point in ever allowing a trade that was in decent profit turn into an overall loss. We can't possible ever *know* what the markets are going to do next, just be able to make a highly informed guess with good probability of success. We may think the planets have lined up in our favour, only to realise we've been mislead by a FO, or that what we thought was an engulf was a flip from some ignored level or SR etc. It's just good sense to take some profit when it's on the table - whether that's in the form of taking most of the trade off at FTA or doing what you prefer and taking only enough profit to cover the SL, setting yourself up for a big payday if the setup works out. And for sure, it's never a bad idea to leave your SL where you originally placed it, since price can so easily return to our entry levels hours or even days after we initially entered (in the case of higher scale setups), only to then later make it to our target. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Smimon's Journal #612 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=600 16/34 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 25 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT juniormaiafx Offline Trader Posts: 103 Thank you received: 138 Same scenario... https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=600 17/34 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 25 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Did you discover the solution to this problem??? QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=600 18/34 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 25 - RTM Community Smimon's Journal #613 HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT smimon Offline Trader Posts: 549 Thank you received: 1075 The "solution", to call it such, would be to understand the move in terms of the higher scales, and also to study the move away from the FO level. Let's say we are coming north strongly out of a W1 FTR, showing a engulf north on the D1 chart, price then "engulfs" south but shoots away from the zone like a rocket - then by combining all pieces of information available to us, we can be pretty certain that it was a FO, not an engulf. Conversely, if there was a W1 eng south in play, price had RT'd up to some D1 bearish FTR, not broken it, and had then come down and "engulfed" the level - then we can be more certain in this case that it was an engulf, and not a FO. Each piece of information the market gives us must be understood in context of the larger scale moves that are going on, and also considering how price approaches the level, and how it moves away. The following user(s) said Thank You: skicker, juniormaiafx QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Smimon's Journal #614 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=600 19/34 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 25 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT smimon Offline Trader Posts: 549 Thank you received: 1075 As a general rule of thumb, something which I attempt to differentiate is: - a) a FL that forms from the engulf of a valid FTR - b) a FL that forms from the engulf of where a retrace started from In the scheme of things, a FL of type (a) is far more important than a FL of type (b). Here is the region of your AU weekly chart, on D1, where I've added some notes. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Smimon's Journal #616 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=600 22/34 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 25 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT smimon Offline Trader Posts: 549 Thank you received: 1075 Well, it's there on the chart really. A bearish FTR has to engulf a bullish level south, make a RT north, and then *make lower lows*. If price comes up north, RTs south, but then *does not make lower lows* and then goes on to *make higher highs*, then it cannot be a bearish FTR by definition. The swing high point is therefore *a place where a RT south started*, as opposed to *the edge of a bearish FTR*. The following user(s) said Thank You: juniormaiafx QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Smimon's Journal #617 Posts: 197 ZeeDeveel Offline Trader Thank you received: 320 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=600 23/34 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 25 - RTM Community smimHoOn MwrEote: AnoEthDeUr CbeAnTeIfOit tNo tradingFOa RlaUrgMe numbeMr oOf RinEstrumenLtsO-GOUT correlations. Think CADJPY is about to go long? Quickly check UCAD - is it at a level it could go short from? Great, take the trade. Is it in the middle of a PAZ? Probably better wait. As discussed previously, individual instruments do not exist in a vacuum. The Fx market is driven by money flowing from one set of currencies into others. It is no coincidence that when an entire currency is about to make a turn, it's major and minors are usually all at significant levels at the same time. In this way we can also usually get a good guess at which directions large news announcements are going to go - 5-10 mins out from GBP news for example, go check the big picture on all GBP crosses, see if they are sat at important levels, or have just turned from important levels 30 mins ago etc. If you're looking at a setup on an individual minor crosspair, and none of the other crosses for this currency are reflecting the same PA or are at good levels, then you should probably wait. I wrote this indi a little while back to help me out with this - it enables me to quickly look at all crosses for a given currency at once, without needing the chart open. It inverts those crosses that need inverting, so that they're all the correct way up with respect to the currency being viewed. i.imgur.com/jBYu4Nx.png QFT "You cant hide billions in a 1min and 5min chart for very long." - A wise doctor 24/34 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=600 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 25 - RTM Community QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE FORUM MORE LOGOUT HOME EDUCATION THANK YOU Smimon's Journal #618 smimon Offline Trader Posts: 549 Thank you received: 1075 I've developed a style which is working well for me - I wait until highest probability levels only, based on higher timeframes and bigger picture analysis, then hit the market with very high risk (10-15%) sized over M10 ATR. This gives me the wiggle room to still get out of the trade with a profit even if my entry level breaks - provided I've waited until MPL to enter. If you jump in before MPL, and the level breaks, you have a far lower chance of getting out. I usually just run the trade to LTF FTA, looking for 1R, or whatever I can get. I have a daily profit TG of 15%, and I can usually hit it in 1 or 2 trades. I have closed out 30 of my last 32 trades in profit using this method - it works. The downside? Well, because I just tend to close out my open trades as soon as I've reached my profit TG for the day, and I'm only entering at best levels, I am leaving a LOT of pips on the table. It is mind bendingly difficult to remain calm when you've got so much risk on the table, and very difficult to not just take your profit when you've hit TG. This is a deliberate strategy however - 15% daily for 66 days compounds �100 into �1,000,000. In all actuality, I need a second account where I take the same entries, but with much lower risk, and run them properly. A different account for a different strategy, I don't believe it does any good to muddle different approaches into the same account. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=600 25/34 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 25 - RTM Community These are the only entries I took today: HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT UCAD M1: i.imgur.com/dSALZeE.png EA M1; i.imgur.com/y80sbX1.png AU M1: i.imgur.com/0s9QSKB.png EG M1: i.imgur.com/ANc9VV4.png (I was stuck on 19.6% and wanted the extra 0.4% for the day!) QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=600 26/34 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 25 - RTM Community Smimon's Journal #620 HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT smimon Offline Trader Posts: 549 Thank you received: 1075 MPL = max pain limit. It basically means the very last possible place price can turn from at the edge of a FTR, the last set of orders. What you'll note is that very often, when an important level is broken, price makes an immediate quick RT back inside. Therefore if you wait only til MPL to enter, with a wider stop, you have every chance to escape what would been a loss with a profit instead. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Smimon's Journal #624 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=600 31/34 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 25 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT smimon Offline Trader Posts: 549 Thank you received: 1075 My trading method is focused on the highest probability events only. A price reaction from just inside MPL of an important FTR, or an engulf of MPL and then quick RT, are very high probability events. It allows me to profit 90% of the time. If price turns some distance away from MPL (although there is usually a good LTF reason for it to do so), then I just let it go without me. No problem, it won't be long to wait for the next chance. If you were to look for shorts immediately when a level breaks like this, then you are essentially breakout trading, which is a high risk approach. Quite often price can make a deep RT after the engulf of a significant level. Safest is to wait for deepest possible RT level, and then enter short. That was just some random PA from UJ M5 months ago. You can find examples of this phenomenon everywhere on any instrument. Just look for how price behaves when it reaches important levels - see how many times you get a valid reaction from FTB, and how many times it engulfs and then starts a RT. Price can also smash straight through the level and not come back, but this generally means you read the HTF picture totally wrong, or were trying to enter from a non- important level. As I've discussed, I devised a trading style whereby I would always enter with a SL based on an ATR multiple to give me wiggle room, and size my risk over this distance. The entry was always to be taken using a button on my LotCalc EA, which would calculate the position size automatically, and place the SL for me. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=625 4/35 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 26 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT The stop was not supposed to be moved, the position was not supposed to be added to, I was to have a daily loss limit, and I even coded rules into my EA to this effect... but I moved my stops, and had an awful habit of popping open the Alt + T quick trade dialog and taking multiple "revenge" positions to try and average out of a bad entry, circumventing all the rules I'd coded. Unhelpfully, this averaging usually worked... but on the odd times I was 100% wrong on the big picture read, price would rip against me, and I would get margin called, wiping out most of the account. I lost count of the number of times this happened. Admittedly, a large part of this was inexperience in trying to enter from levels I'd misanalysed, but there's no point in wiping yourself out just because you're still not the finished article. I did some research online, and find a way to code a C# app which intercepts the Alt + T keypress at a system level, and blocks it. No more quick trade panel. This method of circumventing my rules now being disabled, my EA is able to enforce the "no more than 1 entry", and daily loss limit rules I coded in. I also made my EA log the initial SL position when a trade is taken, and automatically detect when I've moved it, and put it right back again. This now forces me to wait until the best possible place to enter, when I can get my ATR stop behind the next level which can bounce, should the one I am entering from break. I was also having some issues with the steadily increasing balance of my account causing me to become anxious, and affecting my decision making. So to counter this, I now never open the terminal window, so I have nothing more than a rough idea what is in my account and how much � I have in open P/L, and have configured my EA to show me nothing except %age figures. % up/down on the day, % up/down current position, all open positions etc. This way, every single trade is executed (as best as I can fool myself) the same regardless of whether I've got �100 or �100,000 in my account (I wish ;-) ) Anecdotally - I lift weights at the gym a lot. I realised that after you've learned the correct training methods, getting an impressive physique is really 25% time in the gym, and 75% diet. Trading follows a similar ratio for me. Once you've learned how to read a chart, it's 25% https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=625 5/35 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 26 - RTM Community analysis skills, 75% learning to control yourself and not self-sabotage. Getting good at HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT that 75% takes a lot of experience, and even some little hacks and tricks such as I just described. Also key is to realise when you have enough analysis skills to spot the potential for good trades, but it's just your execution that is causing you to fail. At this stage, you are better served by settling on a style you prefer to take your entries and run your trades, and then just repeating it mindlessly for month after month. Only in this way will you start to see consistent improvement. Constantly chopping and changing your approach, or always thinking you "just need to know a bit more information" and scanning the journals of 10 expert level traders (all of whom have years more experience than you) - will in the long run just hold you back from progressing as quickly as you might if you just pick a style and stick with it. Just realise when you are "good enough", and will be better served by more practise, rather than more reading. Well, I'm still not a millionaire. As mentioned previously, my natural temperament is really not suited for trading - overconfident, impulsive, impatient. So as such, curbing this is taking a very long time, one painful iterative step after another. The good news - progress is being made. Every time I make a cock up, I learn a bit more about what I'm good at, and what doesn't suit my personality, and I am able to make steps to adjust and hopefully correct the problem. I am currently, and have been for some time, extremely good at spotting high probability reaction levels in the market. This is without doubt my strongest area. It's the rest of the skillset around this that has been letting me down. I've been "ego trading" for some time now. What's the ultimate goal of trading? Is it to have a high win rate, and stroke your ego? Or is to make money as quickly as possible, regardless of the method provided that it is effective? By ego trading to avoid loss, instead of trading to win, I have been cutting winners short, and really making life hard for myself in terms of having to sustain a high win rate. And in fairness, I can usually hold up a 90% or greater win rate. But when you're only snatching 0.5R off most of these, you feel compelled to up your risk to compensate. Risk 10%, to make 5%, and hope you can keep winning 80-90%. When it works, it's fine. When you start losing a few, it's very tough to handle. I usually 16/35 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=625 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 26 - RTM Community go for 2 weeks without significant loss, and then have a bad day which knocks the HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT wheels off everything. So... first thing is I have to learn to accept loss. And I have to reduce my risk, and aim for at least 2R off every trade. Remember, you only to need to win 33% at 2R to break even, it's not a lot. Second - I simply cannot be trusted to TT. You know how the banks' algos are designed to surge price around in such a manner as to stir up maximal emotion and excitement in the market participants, causing them to act impulsively and without due thought and planning? Well, they certainly work on me. Of course, knowing that more often than not price is just surging into a reversal makes it worse in many ways - as soon as I see a huge surge, all thought goes out the window, and I start looking to fade the thing. Often it works, but when it does not, instant pain. Unplanned trades caused by excitement, impatience. To this end, my EA now only allows POs. I am to maximise profit from my skill in finding very high probability reaction levels, and in this way I will only be taking planned trades. I never have the patience to wait for PA anyway, so at least this way I am ensuring I get best price. At this stage, it's tweaks, not a total overhaul. My live account is not large, but doubling it weekly has become commonplace. In the past, I would unfortunately hand it all back every so often in a disastrous day. Now, I have a daily loss limit in place which I can't break, but I've still had 3 losing days back to back this week, which hasn't happened for a very long time. First day wasn't too bad, second I wiped out within 30 minutes of sitting down trying to fade a surge, and by the third I'd gone to bits chasing stupid PA at stupid levels, taking ridiculous TTs just trying to win it back. We all know how that ends. So I'm upbeat anyway, confident I will sort this out and find the constant account growth consistency which has been eluding me thus far. In other news, I'm working on a really cool piece of software which finds very tightly correlated baskets of crosspairs, and allows you to generate synthetic instruments to use for confluence. It's not uncommon at present for me to be looking for 10-way confluence on a trade decision. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=625 17/35 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 26 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT The market in general is in a very highly correlated state at present, owing to the large financial flows going on between oil, stock indexes, and a large number of currencies. Here's a screenshot of the work in progress. i.imgur.com/1rL64DI.png Basically, by following the chains of positive and negative correlations, and using a bit of deductive logic, it's possible to separate linked correlations into two separate "flows" which do not conflict with one another, and from that provide the ability to generate custom synthetic instruments, one for each currency involved in the basket, and one overall synthetic representing the whole basket. Look at that one at the bottom right, Flow1 - it's a synthetic made up of the average of 15 crosspairs, and it works. When that index turns, so do all of the 15 pairs it is related to at the same time (more or less). These correlations are basically the footprint of pro money in the markets. If you can read a normal chart, you can read the synthetic - it works exactly the same. I'm also currently working on a system to enable me to take basket trades against these synthetics - set an order on the synthetic chart, and when price reaches it, it will automatically take a position long or short on all of the components at the same time. The following user(s) said Thank You: hansma QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Smimon's Journal #667 Posts: 549 smimon Offline Trader 26/39 Thank you received: 1075 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=650 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 27 - RTM Community ThoughHt IO'dMwEait for thEe D"sUpCiriAteTdI"OdNebate to FdOieRdUowMn beforeMcOomRmE enting.LOGOUT Dudu is no fan of RTM, that much is clear at this stage, but I wish him well with his preferred analysis style, if it's making him good, consistent money. That's all we're in this game for at the end of the day. Personally, I love RTM-style analysis. Is it perfect? Is it a panacea? No, not at all. Especially since the markets seems to be getting harder and harder to read recently (or maybe that's just me). It does however remain a sound and effective model to form a basis for looking for levels. When I want to take an entry, I want to do so from a level that has "orders" (I'm not sure this can be thought of in the literal sense, but it's a good analogy for referring to important buying/selling levels on the chart). A FL/FTR that was formed after taking out a key opposing level - well, it's just a very good place to look for orders. Of course FLs/FTRs themselves don't "move the market" - the only thing that moves the market is people actually hitting the market with active buying and selling. I've been using this approach for over a year now, and know that it forms the nucleus of a very successful, accurate method of trading. There are of course many exceptions and nuances, which take a long time to learn, and even when you "know it all", you still face the even larger battle of controlling yourself and your emotions - a battle which I am still nowhere near winning. When I fuck up, I know it's my own fault, and no-one else's. Sometimes I make a total misread, but most of the time I'm just self-sabotaging. There's nothing wrong with the RTM style, provided you realise it's not a set of literal rules which MUST BE OBEYED AT ALL TIMES. Nothing works like that in trading. The reality is, you have to take some of this, some of that, put the pieces together. Which HTF levels are in play, the trend, style of movement, PA, FLs/FTRs, engulfs, key SR (PAZ boundary, QM etc), even a bit of volume profiling, and a huge amount of experience. There are many ways to skin a cat. I like this method because it gives me precision to 27/39 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=650 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 27 - RTM Community anticipate massive price turns within a few pips, if I do my homework correctly. I'm HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT interested in RR, and % on the account. Number of pips gained, number of trades won etc are useless metrics. All that matters is the money going on your account, and the quicker the better The end result is to get rich. However you get there is largely irrelevant. We're all going to have our preferred styles, and be extremely defensive about them, that's just human nature. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE 35/39 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=650 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 27 - RTM Community THANK YOU EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT HOME Smimon's Journal #674 smimon Offline Trader Posts: 549 Thank you received: 1075 Buying/selling symmetry levels are just clear places on the chart where you can see heavy buying/selling took place in the past. Very often, price will make a reaction upon returning to them. If you want to sell for instance, it makes sense to look for levels on the chart where you can see evidence of strong selling in the past. A QM, if you think about it, is just buying/selling symmetry followed by a FO. The "real" orders go in, price then FOs to stop out those with early entries and get some more people going the wrong way, it reverses and an engulf occurs, and then price returns to the "real" level where the first orders went in. As it happens, that level on CJ gave only a very small reaction, which was to be expected really given the strength of the up move. You can't just blindly fade and take FTB to every old FL on every lower timeframe. You have to put everything together in terms of the bigger picture, and learn when it's best to step aside instead of trying to fade, or indeed look for the quick continuation instead. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Smimon's Journal #689 Posts: 549 smimon Offline Trader 22/42 Thank you received: 1075 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=675 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 28 - RTM Community VolumeHpOroMfiliEng is anoEtDheUrCtoAoTl I'OveNstarted FtoOmRaUkMe more uMseOoRf Erecently.LIOf IGcOanUsTee large amounts of activity at a particular price level, inside an important FL, that lines up with some important SR, it's almost a certainty price will react there. Take this level for example (AUDNZD H1, but could be anything): Initially, looks hard to spot a level We draw a custom volume profile around the top part, and immediately a high volume node (HVN) is quite clear. Out of all the accumulation that occurred within this choppy region, these were the price levels which showed the most activity. I believe this is a result of the way the banks' algorithms work - they perhaps instruct them to fill a big order at a certain price, and as price chops around it, it keeps taking small bites until https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=675 23/42 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 28 - RTM Community their position is filled. Normally, we wouldn't be able to see this by eye, but the presence HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT of the HVN gives it away. Now also if we look, there is a clear FL that was faked out, before the move continued. What appears to have happened here - a bull FL was engulfed, and a new bear FL was created, the top edge of this being the key price level. ITs then wanted to load up on positions for a bigger move south, and so price chopped around the area, faking the FL north, for some time, until their positions were filled and price dropped off strongly. The presence of the HVN on the same level as the FOFL (faked-out flag limit) gives us an https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=675 24/42 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 28 - RTM Community A+ very accurate price reaction level. Striving for a strong mind, strong body, and a strong heart. 30/42 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=675 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 28 - RTM Community QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE FORUM MORE LOGOUT HOME EDUCATION THANK YOU Smimon's Journal #696 smimon Offline Trader Posts: 549 Tha Ok, here's how to do it. If you don't have a TradingView subscription, you will only be able to view D1 and above. If yo it's pretty cheap for a year), you can view lower timeframes as well. One thing to note - this calculation isn't perfect. When calculating a composite index for let's s component is raised to the power of a certain weighting. When the base currency of the cross currency (for AUD, this would be a cross like EURAUD), the weighting requires inverting (or u same effect). In addition, the cross candle's high/low values must be flipped. TradingView is n this, the end result being that the candles on lower timeframes get a "hairy" look about them, higher timeframes, this is not as noticeable. The open/close values of the candles are correct The basic approach: - raise each cross component to the power of 1/7 (approx. 0.143) (the sum of all the weights n there are 7 crosses for each currency) - for those crosses where the base currency is the "wrong way around", we take 1/(cross^1/7 - sum all components together Example for AUDX: AUDCAD^0.143+AUDCHF^0.143+1/EURAUD^0.143+1/GBPAUD^0.143+AUDJPY^0.143+AU 3 If you paste that into the symbol bar in TradingView, you end up with a chart which looks like https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=675 31/42 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 28 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=675 32/42 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 28 - RTM Community The AUDX produced by my indicator in MT4 looks like this: HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT So as you can see, pretty similar. 33/42 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=675 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 28 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT This should be enough info for you to recreate the other average indexes. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Smimon's Journal #698 smimon Offline Trader Posts: 549 Thank you received: 1075 I'm always playing with various new tools I've coded up to help me make better decisions on which crosspairs to hit. When you trade using 8 average currency indexes as your basis, you are essentially trading all 28 crosses in the market. A big part of trading successfully intraday is the ability to be familiar with your chosen instruments, to hold the story of how the various higher timeframes are fitting together into a coherent map in your mind. The brain is only of limited capacity however, and whilst you might be able to do this for 4-8 instruments, you'd have to be an Einstein level genius to manage it for 28. To confuse things further, moves of differing sizes are always happening simultaneously on different scales. GBPUSD might be the strongest pair in the market for the week, but chopping on the day, and actually the weakest over the last 2 hours. Again if you only trade a small number of instruments, this is easier to keep track of in your mental map, but when you trade the same small set of instruments every day you run the risk of just staring at hours of chop some days when all the big moves are elsewhere in the market. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=675 35/42 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 28 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT I came close to a grand unified theory of tracking currency correlations a while back, which enabled me to trade baskets of 10 or more crosspairs from a single synthetic instrument, but ultimately had to abandon it, despite several times successfully predicting the daily move of 2/3 of the market. I was basically trading a brand new set of instruments and synthetic charts every single day, and just couldn't keep up with the ever changing nature of it. So as it is, I have settled on using the 8 average currency indexes as my basis for all entries. I can just about manage to keep up with everything this way. My basic strategy is to fade nice approaches to key FTB levels, ideally as HTF trend continuations, but even against trend will yield some few pips 90% of the time or more. Even this has its complications however. Let's say GBPX is surging long towards a level you want to fade - which crosses to hit? You have a choice of 7. If you pick only one, you risk selecting the worst one of the bunch which goes nowhere, due to the other currency component not showing any strength against GBP in that moment, even as all the other crosses move as expected. It's actually amazing how often this seems to happen, sod's law I guess. The safest idea is to hedge your bets across 2-3 crosses which are showing the most strength/weakness against GBP at that moment in time, since logically, these should be the ones which reflect the bounce in the most promising fashion. To that end, I knocked up a simple tool yesterday which allows me to draw a box around an area of price on my currency index, and see currency & cross performance graphs for that section of time. As always, pictures are worth a thousand words, so I'll show a quick example. This was GBPX M5 as we were coming up to London open on Friday morning. Price was moving strongly towards a key HTF FL, FTB. This is always especially nice to see 5-10 minutes out from 0800, since very often the volatility from London open gives you a nice move. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=675 36/42 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 28 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=675 37/42 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 28 - RTM Community Which crosses to fade it against though? You can always do a visual inspection of the HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT other 7 currency charts, but this is quite a lot to manage in the heat of the moment. So, using the new tool, I would have seen this: This is a performance graph of the period of time encompassing the 6 or so M5 bars which comprised the surge into the level on GBPX. Using this, I now can very quickly see that USD, CHF and EUR were the 3 strongest currencies in the market during this time, and so the 3 trades to take would have been: short GU, short GCHF, long EG. I'd of course have wanted to make sure that these 3 were at some sort of semi-logical place for a price bounce to happen. There is one drawback to this method however - sometimes when an entire currency 38/42 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=675 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 28 - RTM Community makes a HTF turn, the money flows out of it into a different currency which right up until HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT the turn was the weakest currency in the market. But you can't predict this reliably (or I've not figured out how to yet, I suspect you could use short-term correlations for a clue), and it's adding extra complication to the method. Often if you've zoned out during the day and an alert that you'd set earlier goes off, you only have 5 minutes to make a snap decision, so you want the process to be as simple as possible. Attachments: The following user(s) said Thank You: Ligo, duy90 QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Smimon's Journal #699 Posts: 10 T Ligo Offline Trader smimon wrote: Ok, here's how to do it. 39/42 Example for AUDX: https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=675 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 28 - RTM Community AUDCAD^0.143+AUDCHF^0.143+1/EURAUD^0.143+1/GBPAUD^0.143+AUDJPY^0.143+ HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT 43 which one more accurate if i use like this? and why? Obviously AUDEUR and AUDGBP don't exist as real instruments. That one is better since it gets around the issue of not being able to invert the candle high/lows I mentioned previously, and will give you a cleaner chart. Good spot. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Smimon's Journal #707 smimon Offline Trader Posts: 549 Thank you received: 1075 Excuse my rant the other day, frustrations just got on top of me. There's really no need to be able to read every single move on every chart, just that we can spot and profit from the most obvious setups. I've put a string of large profit days together since then, all is well. I've been thinking about a way to reduce the risk of my preferred "unconfirmed FTB to key MPL" strategy. 70-80% of the time, it works, you get a nice clean bounce, and an easy ride to at least LTF FTA. Sometimes however, when the trend is really against you, price will bounce only a very small distance, won't even reach LTF FTA, and then continue brutally the way it was https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=700 9/35 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 29 - RTM Community going, leaving you badly offside (I don't really use a SL apart from to size my initial HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT entry, I prefer to always try to manage an average position back onside). You can minimise the risk of this happening by making sure that: a) you are not trying to fade one of the strongest intraday trends in the whole market (strongest vs weakest currencies) b) you analyse the chart properly to make sure that if the level you are attempting to enter from breaks, there is somewhere nearby of some importance which has a very good chance to bounce price However, accidents do happen. From experience of doing this for a long time now, there are few scenarios which can play out when price surges into a fresh important FL: a) v-shaped clean bounce straight to FTA (does not happen often) b) initial clean bounce which stops some distance short of FTA. Price then comes back to re-test the initial entry level, or forms a LTF QM, stopping out those who entered early, before then going on to make it all the way to FTA, and often beyond c) initial bounce which stops short of FTA, turns around and keeps going I'm in the business of trying to "never lose" an overall trade sequence, whilst trying not to cheat myself out of profit by being too cautious. Obviously, we don't want to go b/e on our initial entry unless we've managed to get in with 0-1 pip accuracy on a massive M1 rejection candle. Similarly, I don't want to set a SL outside the zone, get an initial bounce, and then have price just keep going and stop me out. I'm also not a huge fan of taking out enough of the position to cover the SL at the first chance, because it really gimps profit if the trade turns into a big runner. Running full position as far as possible is key. What I aim to do from next week is: 1) hit the initial surge into MPL, as close to MPL as possible, with decent size 2) at the very first indication that the initial bounce is faltering, drop the whole thing 3) this is now "the market's money", which I can use as "armour" if you like to protect my next entry 4) I then wait for price to come back and make a double top/bottom, or a LTF QM, and I https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=700 10/35 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 29 - RTM Community throw all the money I just banked at the trade, using a fixed, tight SL HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT 5) Now I've got the best of both worlds. A no-risk trade, with much bigger lot than I originally was willing to risk. I also now have the chance to just walk away and let the thing run, if it is showing good signs it wants to This is quite a bit to think about in the moment, so I'll code something into my EA to help out with it. The downside is that I will potentially cut myself out of any v-shape reversals without properly running it, but this doesn't happen that often, and I'm primarily interested in reducing the riskiness of my approach. (I hope you don't take this message the wrong way.) https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=700 14/35 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 29 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT The following user(s) said Thank You: cuimingzhao QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Smimon's Journal #711 smimon Offline Trader Posts: 549 Thank you received: 1075 Anticipating a news setup using currency index confluence: It's a safe bet to assume most economic announcements are always leaked. This usually presents us with good opportunities to predict the direction the news will go, since we understand the tricks the ITs like to play in order to get people offside. Every week or two, GBP has 3 days in the week where medium importance economic announcements come out at 0930 GMT. Whilst these rarely produce huge moves, they always increase GBP volatility for 30-60 mins afterwards, and present nice opportunities for quick profit. The general approach is: - check the currency index for the relevant currency. See where it is. Has it engulfed a level 30 mins earlier, and is making a RT? Is it sat at a strong level showing rejection PA 10-15 mins ahead? Is it making a straight line move into a key level? Is it mid-paz, but moving strongly already in direction of HTF trend? - check the other currency indexes to find the best opposing currency to hit it against. This can be either one which is itself at a key level and can bounce, or one that is https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=700 15/35 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 29 - RTM Community already moving strongly in the correct direction as compared to the direction we want to HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT trade GBP, with room to run - from the 2-3 crosspairs you have perhaps filtered down to, perform the same checks as on the indexes. Levels, PA etc - wait until 5-10 mins before the news to enter. Most of the time you can enter 10 seconds before the news hits. Ideally, you want to see a LTF surge going *against* the direction you think the news will go, right up until it hits. This is usually a trick to make people think the news is going to go the other way. Another type of very nice setup we can see, is price making an uninterrupted surge for 30 minutes right into the news, into a key level. So, for today's 0930 GBP news, we start with GBPX M15 As we can see here, GBPX is currently in a range, held by 2 FLs above and below. The HTF trend is down, so it is more likely the lower one will break. However, with 15 mins to go before the news, GBPX was sat right at the bottom of the range, and had not broken it. In my experience, it is extremely unusual for price to be sat at a valid level, https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=700 16/35 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 29 - RTM Community and for a news surge to suddenly break it *and keep going*. Much more likely, is that an HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT engulf has already happened in the lead up to the news, and then the news continues the move; or the initial news stab breaks the level, but then price RTs sharply the other way, to complete the move at a later date. This chart favoured GBP going long. (remember, we're not necessarily looking for a huge move, just to ride the volatility for 5-10 mins after the news hits. We can use our understanding of the bigger picture to inform a decision as to whether it's worth trying for a longer hold or not) I won't post all the other currency indexes, but the one that caught my eye at this time was USDX: USD had what appeared to be a short term trend reversal day yesterday, unrelenting buying pressure. However, we had reacted to a key FL overnight, hit support, and then made a clean push deeper into it. Whilst you couldn't bet on this reversing the move short, it was an important level, and the 2TB offered the chance of secondary, smaller bounce. This chart supported USD selling off for a short period of time. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=700 17/35 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 29 - RTM Community Combining the 2, we deduce that GU is a good pair to try and go long on for the news. HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT So the next step is to look at the chart of GU itself. This a section of an GU H1 chart. What catches the eye is a decent DP, which was spiked through very quickly, circled (I believe this was FOMC last week that did this). There is a decent chance that orders were not filled at this level, so we can realistically expect some sort of bounce upon return. The slightly higher line is key SR from a PAZ boundary. Price was sat just above the lower line 10-15 mins out from the news. So we now have 2 currency indexes and the cross itself supporting the possibility of a short-term long move from the news. We would not really expect a large move, because the HTF pictures on GBPX and USDX do not support it at this time. The final step is to examine M5/M15 PA for the candles leading up to 0930. Here is GU M5: https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=700 18/35 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 29 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Circled, we can see price has surged down strongly (herd is now selling) and is showing nice M5 rejection PA from a place we anticipated in the period 0915-0925. Seeing this, I entered 10 minutes out from the news, keeping an eye on proceedings. Had price started to shoot up with just a few minutes to go, I most likely would have exited, since as mentioned this is usually a contrarian indicator. However, as it was, the 0925 candle, the last one before the news, closed bearish, which was enough confidence for me to hold into the release. Finally M1 of how the trade played out: 19/35 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=700 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 29 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT On this timeframe, we can actually see a small FL just below the very obvious SR line. This made for a good initial target (and indeed, final target in this case), since most of the herd will be expecting price to reach this SR line, and turning price before it gets there represents a good opportunity for the ITs to confuse. The news itself gapped open slightly lower, before recovering strongly, and coming through for a nice quick 25 pips in 10 minutes. Seems like a lot of work for 25 pips? Perhaps, but when you consider a) with enough time and experience, this entire mental process can be performed in under 60 seconds b) putting all of the factors together, we end up with a probability of being correct most likely in excess of 90% https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=700 20/35 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 29 - RTM Community then I believe it's worth it. HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Although today, this move was small and against the larger picture, it was still extremely predictable, and using the method outlined above, on many other days with other news setups, there will be a lot more on the table. Let's say you're the best trader in the world. Let's say that all your entries are picture perfect. You never know. Something really bad can still happen at any moment. When trading any crosspair, it's important to consider whether it's moving in one direction solely because of the strength or weakness of only one of its constituents. ITs often like to play currencies as a whole to my knowledge, rather than focusing on individual pairs. The best trades come when one part is weak and the other part is strong, resulting in swift movements. CJ and UJ obviously share JPY as a common component. Any strength in JPY will therefore push them lower. However, CAD and USD are almost opposing currencies - over 80% of the value of the trade-weighted CADX index comes from USDCAD. Therefore usually as USD moves up, CAD moves down, and vice versa. For this reason, we can see that the only way CJ and UJ will both go down convincingly is in the event of significant JPY strength. If JPY remains neutral, then the sole driver of the pairs will be USD, meaning that if UJ starts going down in the face of JPY neutrality, then CJ will start going up. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=75 18/29 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 4 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Which is essentially what's happening to my 2 trades up the moment, see-sawing off each other. A strong kick from JPY will push them both down, but I've effectively bet 50% against myself. A good way to look at things is in triplets of pairings. Let's say EURUSD is going down in HTF trend - we therefore know that USD is stronger than EUR. Let's also say however that GBPUSD is headed up in HTF trend. GBP is stronger than USD. Logic then dictates that the strongest moves should be coming short on EURGBP. USD is stronger than EUR, but GBP is stronger than USD, and so therefore far stronger than EUR. This is of course all academic, and it's possible to trade the currency pairs perfectly well in isolation, but it's always worth bearing in mind so you not only don't bet against yourself, but also so that you can pick up on something that's really looking like it's going to run. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Smimon's Journal #95 geejay Offline Trader PRICE ACTION WILL SHOW YOU THE WAY Posts: 41 Thank you received: 63 smimon wrote: Things I've learned this week: including the supply/demand zone that formed the level you are trying to trade o within your drawn zones eliminates any chance of being caught by the FO, at https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=75 22/29 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 4 - RTM Community the expense of a wider stop - but better to have the win for 1 or 2R less, than HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT getting stopped out and getting nothing Have a good weekend all. I wanted to add some ideas for consideration on this one. I can see two options that allows one to be conservative but help maximize returns. 1. Expect the FO zone on the other side of the supply/demand and only trade that. In other words, instead of picking the entry in the supply zone, pick the entry in the FO zone above the supply. You will missing some trades but you will have a smaller stop loss to deal with. 2. The other idea is a hybrid of your style and my #1. Trade a portion of your risk at the supply zone and another portion in the FO zone. I spent a lot of time reading the FO or Engulf thread in the Homework room and it dawned on me that often price will FO to the decision point on the other side of the most recent supply/demand zone. So, why not play 2 trades. One at the edge of the obvious zone (S or D) and another in the DP beyond the S/D zone. Essentially if you had $100 to risk per trade and you see a nice supply zone with a FO area above, well, you take a $50 position near the edge of the supply zone and another $50 near the DP of the FO zone. That way you get a chance for 2 tickets to the party. Hope that makes sense. GJ. Please note that I am still demo trading and not being very successful picking entries but I think I am pretty good at money management and so I hope this helped. The following user(s) said Thank You: smimon www.myfxbook.com/portfolio/mt4-4013665/499286 QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Smimon's Journal #118 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=100 21/39 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 5 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT smimon Offline Trader Posts: 549 Thank you received: 1075 Quick something I knocked up to monitor my average position when adding to a trade in progress, and show me where multiplying my total lots by a factor of x will put my average position. It moves up and down as the bid price moves up and down. The idea is that I wait for a sufficiently deep retrace that I can double my lots (or whatever) and keep my average position behind a "safe" extreme - typically a FTR, the engulfing of which would mean I would consider the trade dead in the water. Once a trade is in progress and you double (or whatever) up, you essentially go double or quits. You're either out for a lot more at your target, or you lose the lot at B/E at your new average position level if price reaches it. It's a concept called "Space management" I found on another forum some time ago. The idea isn't new of course. If you're feeling really bold, and your trade has run almost all the way to target, you can do something silly like x10 your lots for the final stretch, "compressing" your space. You can also remove lots to a trade in progress if it looks like it's getting into trouble, putting some in the bank, and moving your average position further out, "expanding" your space, and giving yourself more room to survive a deep RT. If the RT ends up continuing back in your original trade direction, you can "compress" the space back again. It's more suited for swing or position type trades I think, running over many hours, days, or even weeks, but you can still use it on a LTF trade, as I am attempting to do here. Something I hope to master, is if I get on something that goes on a good run over several hours, is to "compress" up my space upon every successful retest and hold of freshly created FLs after engulfs. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=100 22/39 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 5 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Attachments: QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=100 23/39 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 5 - RTM Community Smimon's Journal #119 HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT smimon Offline Trader Posts: 549 Thank you received: 1075 Unfortunately all the fancy position management tools in the world can't guard against idiocy. Quite often we seem to see these indexes move in sync with one another, with the strength of the moves varying to one degree or another. Many of the candles are blurred and fuzzy, which represents times in which the indices were moving around out of sync with one another. However, at other times, clear moves appear on the index, showing us that co-ordinated buying or selling was taking place across all 4. At 1830ish yesterday, we can see the following setup on this "index" index, which came to and end at about 1400 today: https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=100 27/39 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 5 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT And here, I'm going to show the FTSE, DAX, S&P and Dow (in that order) with the same two time periods marked: https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=100 28/39 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 5 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=100 29/39 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 5 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=100 30/39 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 5 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Pretty neat huh? To think, each one of these indexes is itself a weighted representation of at least 30 share prices, or 100 or 500 share prices in the case of two of them. So in order for a composite index created from these 4 to all move in sync like this, it means that co-ordinated buying and selling of 100s of shares is going on across 3 different countries at very precise times. And some people say the market is random... Hopefully I can use this moving forwards to give me some good heads up on when the indexes are about to shift. Now that my chart reading skills have improved quite a bit, I'm planning to start looking at my GBPX, JPYX etc indices again. I'd dropped them for a while because they were confusing me - they behave almost like normal pairings in the moves that they make, but sometimes a little oddly.
