UJ has currently come short off such a level - the hollow box at the top is the first lower TF FTR at the edge of a prominent D1 FTR. Here's the daily chart, and why I think it is important: https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=125 23/60 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 6 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT And here's M15 of that area, showing the level we've recently had a short reaction to: https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=125 24/60 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 6 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Now, like good traders, we don't just dive in immediately, but we wait for an engulf of a significant opposing FTR to give us some idea that the move has got legs, and then enter on a RT. Referring back to the topmost chart, just below this level we're currently having a long reaction to, is a level I've marked W1S. It was a weekly supply extreme, which in fact formed from the daily FTR we've now had a move short off. The initial move down from the top engulfed both the FTR from the upwards engulf of 25/60 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=125 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 6 - RTM Community this W1S, and also the source of the move that engulfed the W1S. After travelling a little HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT further down, we've had a strong retrace off the level that was the source of the engulf of the short level I've marked "1st FTR" - this was the 1st large FTR formed after the top of the W1S when we fell away from it way back in 2007. Now, to my mind, the level we are currently coming long off is less "significant" in the scheme of things. The W1S was a weekly supply extreme, and so presumably required a good shove to push through. We then took out this "shove" level from the first drop. What I'm thinking is that price is now likely to RT back to the first new FTR formed from the recent drop (marked on the chart, also a QM level), which should then open the way up for a drop down to at least the level I've marked "Eng src of next FTR" - it being, as the name suggests, the source of the move which pushed through the next FTR in line from the down move in 2007. However, time and again, I've seen these seemingly "innocuous" levels actually turn into massive trend continuations. I'm wondering - is it just down to my currently flawed understanding of the market that I get things like this wrong? Or am I just being far too ambitious in even trying to predict what level we're going to fall down to, given that the current level we've reacted long from hasn't actually been engulfed yet? I presume the concepts of PAZ apply on weekly and daily charts, just the same as they do on H4, H1, M30 etc, it's just that it takes a lot longer to play out. I'm sure it's possible to rate the relative importance of each FTR on the scheme of things - whether it was a daily/weekly level, where it formed, what the move from that level took out, and thereby derive an understanding of where we think price is likely to go. I'm just perhaps not very good at it yet, and require more chart time and seeing things play out in order to get better at the analysis. Where are we likely to RT to, if we've formed a nice short move off a HTF FTR? A fresh H4 supply level formed near the edge. I've seen a lot of the time, that if you draw a line from the body close of the candle prior to the momo candle (the two taken together defining the S/D zone), it gives you a good idea of where price might bounce from, allowing you to hone in on any important LTF levels around the vicinity. Here is a chart showing some H4 supply zones that were created as UJ moved short from the top, with lines drawn from the body closes of the previous candles. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=125 30/60 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 6 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Here are the same lines, but shown on M15 - helpful to narrowing our levels down. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=125 31/60 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 6 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT They also usually form nice SR lines we can watch for reactions to in the future. You can also drop down through the timeframes, H1, M30 etc, and repeat the same technique to find the H1/whatever fresh supply zones "inside" the H4 supply zone, narrowing the range of nice RT levels even further. Also, if the level has already had a wick through it, but hasn't been taken out, it can still be used as a point of reference in the future. Here's an example from GJ: https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=125 32/60 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 6 - RTM Community H1 HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT LTF 33/60 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=125 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 6 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT I believe this works because really what you're seeing when you draw these body lines is minor SR flip zones, or in fact the other side of ignored LTF FTRs, that are subsequently momo'd over when price makes a big surge. This doesn't always work of course - it depends entirely on whether the H4 (or whatever) S/D level is actually significant in the scheme of things, depending on what we're coming off on Daily, what we've engulfed etc. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=125 34/60 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 6 - RTM Community Anyway, excuse my ramblings, I still sometimes feel like I'm failing to see the wood for HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT the trees, but I know it'll all gel perfectly well given more chart time. Hit my 10% daily target in 20 mins after sitting down, want to go do something else, that'll do me https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=150 23/55 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 7 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=150 24/55 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 7 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Attachments: The following user(s) said Thank You: kkb, briantv, Ywicked, Experiorz, tibioza QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=150 25/55 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 7 - RTM Community SmiHmOoMnE's JourEnDaUlCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT #166 smimon Offline Trader Posts: 549 Thank you received: 1075 For me, there are only 2 things I need to take a trade: - a zone that could turn price, be it a FL, FTR, QM, whatever - a reason to enter the trade Finding zones is what RTM is all about. The excellent analysis techniques provide us with the means to identify possible turning points ahead of time. We could just set POs, and trust that more will work than will not, which is no doubt a profitable way of trading provided you're getting at least 2 or 3R on your trades. But if we really want to bump up our win %age, we need a good reason to enter the trade at the zone, merely apart from the fact that price has arrived there. What's a good reason? PA of course. We let the market tell us the intentions of the ITs. What's a good type of PA to look out for? For me at least, it's the initial surge from a level that engs something significant, and then returns to source. That's where we enter. I like to use D1/H4/H1 for my zones, and M15/M5 for my entry PA. Once you get used to spotting these types of trades, they're everywhere. Some may only make a small surge up to the next zone, some may keep going for much longer - that's when your HTF analysis comes into play. To my mind though, I'm quite happy grabbing 3 or 4R off a good surge in 30 mins. If I want to hit the equivalent of 20R on a trade, I've got 2 options - leave it running for ages, and risk my HTF analysis being wrong, or if I'm confident in my win %age, rolling the profits of the first trade into a second. If I lose, then I'm just back to where I started https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=150 26/55 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 7 - RTM Community for the day. If I win the second, that's getting on for some decent account gain, HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT depending on your risk level. I like to trade between 2 and 4% risk depending on how much I like the look of things. If I hit 3R off a 4% trade (12%), and then roll that into a second and do it again (12 x 3 = 36, plus the original 12 = 48%) then I've almost increased my account by 50% just off 2 single 3R trades, which can usually play out in an hour or less. I've even written it down so I can see it in front of me - "3R, and then 3R, that's all you need". Obviously you need to be quite consistent to get this to work well, which is why I use the two steps mentioned above. You can be in and out, with your 3/5/10/20/50% whatever, off a couple of trades in less than an hour, and then go and do something else with your day. I first learned of this idea a while back, when I was still in the wilderness before RTM, from a great trader on another forum, who called the concept a "zero line" (or "z line" for short), because it's quite frequently the point where breakout traders will have their trades zeroed out by a RT after they've moved their stops to B/E, before their stops are hit and add fuel to the fire, and the move continues on its way. We can't just use them blindly, there has to exist a confluence of factors to strengthen the decision. And of course, obviously, they do just represent a place within a supply or demand zone. But they represent a great place for us to start narrowing down our search for pinpoint trades. I've seen price bounce off them almost to the pip so many times over the time I've been trading, that it would be foolish of me not to include them in my toolkit. Why do they work? Most of the time, if you draw them in and drop timeframe, you'll find that what has been marked is actually some local SR which held price up or down for a while before a surge, or also quite often the edge of the last LTF FTR which get's momo'd over by a surge, which then becomes an ignored SD level. Both of these things we know are usually good for a bounce. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=150 43/55 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 7 - RTM Community Here's a daily chart of NU, where I've marked in some z lines and shown where price HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT reacted to them. You can find them on any timeframe, but obviously the higher timeframe ones are more probable for big bounces. Here's today's NU surge and drop on LTF. 44/55 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=150 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 7 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT When it comes down to it, it's all just boxes (FL/FTR SD zones) and lines (SR, z lines etc). Why anyone would waste time with squiqqly line indicators all over their chart is beyond me. After falling afoul of losing sight of the big picture last week, I've been messing around with a good way to superimpose HTF S/D zones on top of each other, in a clear, easily visually identifiable manner. I was previously using outline boxes with borders of various thickness, but I quickly ended up with a confusing mess of lines on my chart. As I'm sure anyone who has experimented with drawing rectangles on top of each other in MT4 knows, it's got an annoying habit of flipping colours of overlapping shapes, so https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=150 49/55 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 7 - RTM Community that you end up with weird and wonderful shades, none of which was what you HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT originally wanted. It's possible to apply a bitwise negation to a rectangle's colour before drawing it in order to counter this effect, but you still run into problems when one part of a rectangle overlaps another, and one part does not. The only way I could reliably find to achieve the effect I was looking for was to convert the rectangle's price/time co-ordinates to x/y using the built in function, and then draw a rectangle label exactly on top of the original one. Rectangle labels do not suffer from the aforementioned colour flipping problem, but they are fixed in position - as you scroll the chart, they remain fixed in the same place. As such, you have to implement a timer function to redraw them every couple of hundred milliseconds in the correct position, which is a bit of a bodge (there is a lag effect as you scroll the chart quickly), but works. Also some other small caveats like making sure you sort your list of rectangles into descending order of timeframe (W1 then D1 then H4 etc) so that the correct drawing order is respected. All a bit geeky, but I'm still a software developer at heart, and I always like trying to come up with creative solutions to work around the limitations of software. The end result is quite nice - here's a recent chart of GBPCHF, where we can see price hitting the nested H1/H4/D1/W1 supply zones, and falling off nicely. Next up on the list is a way for me to quickly indicate which zones are engulf sources, and getting some good colours to indicate engulfed zones on various TFs. The more information I can assimilate from a single glance at a chart, the quicker I can make decisions about which direction to try and look for entries on LTF, and trade accordingly. As an aside, this approach also lends itself quite nicely to a S/D radar, which will show me, once the zones are drawn, at a glance for all pairs I trade which ones are currently in tradeable zones. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=150 50/55 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 7 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Attachments: 51/55 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=150 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 7 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT The following user(s) said Thank You: kkb, tibioza QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Smimon's Journal #175 schaefsky Offline Trader Posts: 22 Thank you received: 15 So you are a software developer too, that might explain why I like to read your journal so much! The following user(s) said Thank You: kkb, schaefsky, tibioza QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Smimon's Journal #182 Posts: 549 smimon Offline Trader 9/48 Thank you received: 1075 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=175 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 8 - RTM Community Eng or FHOO?ME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Here's something which was puzzling me for a while, the number of times that a FL appears to have been engulfed, only for the move to continue in its original direction - seemingly a "failed engulf". In terms of a bullish move, consider that many times a FL forms just after a FTR is momo'd over, or ignored. When price retraces later, and appears to have engulfed our FL, in actual fact what has happened is that it has dipped slightly lower to bounce off the ignored level that the FL formed above. This will of course be clearer with an example. Here's a recent one on EU. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=175 10/48 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 8 - RTM Community It may be the case that there's something on the tick chart in these occasions, but I HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT don't have the data. I'm not going to treat any eng of a few pixels as a real eng - there are too many reasons why it might be FO. When a genuine eng happens, it's usually by a lot more than a pip or two - and even if you miss a few valid ones, you'll keep yourself out of a lot of bad ones too. You can toggle whether you want the line to be drawn from the candle wick or the candle body with a hotkey. The following user(s) said Thank You: kkb, tibioza QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Smimon's Journal #196 smimon Offline Trader Posts: 549 Thank you received: 1075 First journal update of the new year, so I'll take the opportunity to wish you all a Happy New Year. So my first 3 months with the RTM material were a real ray of light in the darkness of the "trading without a clue" wilderness that had come before. I'd gained the ability to make some great trades, but was still haemorrhaging losers all over the place. After https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=175 29/48 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 8 - RTM Community once already revisiting all the material once from scratch, I decided to read through the HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT RTM thread on Forex Factory to see if I could glean any further nuggests from it. It's a great read, I recommend it to anyone who hasn't seen it yet. The main thing I picked up was the importance of FLs above all else. So many times a seemingly strong FTR gets taken out, only for you to then realise we just went deeper into the FL that caused it. In particular, this sentence from IF about FOs struck home and made a lot of sense - "Say price is coming up to a supply. The only way we can be sure that supply will hold price down is if that supply has already visited the extreme set of orders in the flag limit to its left. If the supply fell short of that, we can anticipate a FO to those extreme orders." (the link to the thread is here by the way - www.forexfactory.com/showthread.php?t=420159 ) With this information in mind, and resolving that this year I will turn this trading style into a science, rather than an art, I'm trying to take fewer trades, and analyse the charts as thoroughly as possible to spot the important FLs, and engulfs thereof. I'm sure I'll still get some wrong, but I aim to get those down to the utter minimum. It really is possible not to take any full stop outs if you know what you're doing. After a couple of failed entries on other pairs which went out for b/e + commission, here's a trade I took on UJ today which played out well, with reasons shown on the charts. 1. I simply wait for an engulf of an important FL (making sure we've checked on M1 if it's real, or a fakeout), and then assume price is going to reach the other side of the PAZ down (or up) to the next important FL on whatever TF we're on. It's then a case of waiting through the RT after the engulf until it gets to a likely level, and entering. Even better is when it reaches the last possible level that it can react to before it has to continue on its way. If you've read your engulfs correctly, those are almost a guaranteed trade, and the depth of the RT opens up a ton of space to trade into. 2 PAZs might only be 10 pips apart, but when you've had a 50 pip RT after an engulf, you've got a nice trade. Longer term bias doesn't necessarily matter for a single trade - a valid setup is a valid setup after all - but if we're coming off a daily or weekly level, in line with the HTF trend, then I am more likely to hold on to a trade and let it run as far as possible. This is something I need to work on more really - my UJ trade went much further, but I'm still finding my feet, so when I've got good profit on the table, I tend to bag it quickly. The confidence for longer holds will come with time. With counter trend trades, I will take everything at the first target, no exceptions, and then look to reverse if possible - the target for one trade is often a great place to enter an opposing one. With trades that go with the long term trend, then it's up to your discretion whether you want to keep hold of it in the hope it will blast through a few more levels - you can always monitor PA and get out when it looks like you've had an important FL eng go against you. Bear in mind too that PAZs exist on W1, D1, H4 etc etc. Which PAZ we've engulfed into on which timeframe is a good way to give ourselves a directional compass for our https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=200 2/45 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 9 - RTM Community longer term bias. EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT HOME Hope that helps in some way. The following user(s) said Thank You: kkb, Crisaor, Fader, tibioza QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Smimon's Journal #202 smimon Offline Trader Posts: 549 Thank you received: 1075 Here's a quick example for you, EJ. I simply wait for an engulf of an important FL (making sure we've checked on M1 if it's real, or a fakeout), and then assume price is going to reach the other side of the PAZ down (or up) to the next important FL on whatever TF we're on. It's then a case of waiting through the RT after the engulf until it gets to a likely level, and entering. Even better is when it reaches the last possible level that it can react to before it has to continue on its way. If you've read your engulfs correctly, those are almost a guaranteed trade, and the depth of the RT opens up a ton of space to trade into. 2 PAZs might only be 10 pips apart, but when you've had a 50 pip RT after an engulf, you've got a nice trade. Longer term bias doesn't necessarily matter for a single trade - a valid setup is a valid setup after all - but if we're coming off a daily or weekly level, in line with the HTF trend, then I am more likely to hold on to a trade and let it run as far as possible. This is something I need to work on more really - my UJ trade went much further, but I'm still finding my feet, so when I've got good profit on the table, I tend to bag it quickly. The confidence for longer holds will come with time. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=200 11/45 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 9 - RTM Community With counter trend trades, I will take everything at the first target, no exceptions, HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT and then look to reverse if possible - the target for one trade is often a great place to enter an opposing one. With trades that go with the long term trend, then it's up to your discretion whether you want to keep hold of it in the hope it will blast through a few more levels - you can always monitor PA and get out when it looks like you've had an important FL eng go against you. Bear in mind too that PAZs exist on W1, D1, H4 etc etc. Which PAZ we've engulfed into on which timeframe is a good way to give ourselves a directional compass for our longer term bias. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Smimon's Journal #215 smimon Offline Trader Posts: 549 Thank you received: 1075 Personally, I only draw the lowest or highest edges of a bullish or bearish flag respectively, rather than a box around the whole thing, since it seems to me that these are the important edges, but I'm sure there's a reason why most of the academy students draw the entire box. There are tons of examples in my journal. This is an example of one of the charts I trade from. These levels have just accumulated over time, I occasionally have to spring clean my charts to clean them up. There are probably more levels on here than I need, but I'm still in the process of refining my identification of which levels matter most. i.imgur.com/EFkP3XO.png I try to find only the important engulfed flags/FTRs, and nothing else. I find them by 25/45 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2141-smimon-s-journal?start=200 7/27/2018 Smimon's Journal - Page 9 - RTM Community looking left, and seeing what engulfed what. The HTF engulfed flags (shown in grey HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT with a red or green "tab" at the end) are more important, the LTF engulfed flags (greyish dark green or red) act as guidelines to how we're going to get from FTR to FTR. I will use HTF initially to find the more prominent FLs/FTRs - but as soon as I've marked it on H4/D1/W1 whatever, I drop on to M1/M5, and extend the zone to the left until I find the actual FL it formed off. In this way, I know straight away which are the more important flags I should be looking at. I'll use PAZ formed by FTRs corresponding to H1/H4/D1 FL engulfs as a directional guideline, and the LTF FLs as a way of tracking how I'm going to get there, and which level we're likely to RT from. Sorry if this is a bit confusing, but it's quite hard to summarise in a few paragraphs a process that it's taken me 3 months of staring at charts about 10 hours a day to figure at. It's really all about just starting with the basic building blocks, the engulf and the FTR, and then applying it over and over again in your chart history, until it's become second nature. I must have marked up a 1000 different places on various charts at one point or another. Sometimes if I'm bored, I just scroll back to 2009 or another random year, and start making on where I think the important levels are, and then scroll forward in time to see if I was right. Alternatively, just find somewhere a massive move originated from, draw a horizontal line from the top/bottom, and then scroll back until you find the place it started from, and see if you can figure out why. How you HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT handle it in your trading? The FTR is always the important level that we must consider the engulf of when defining a FTR PAZ to trade with. The DP to engulf is quite often an important level in its own right, and we can quite often see a big bounce from these levels, but it's the FTRs and the engulfs of the FTRs (not forgetting to include the FL that caused the FTR within our zone) that give us our heads up on how to trade. The only caveat to consider is if the DP to engulf is also an important FTR in its own right. I can show you a quick example from UJ from memory (excuse the small text, I had to resize chart, it should still be legible): (edit: just noticed (3) seems to be missing.
